== v23.07 ==
All Buff / Boss modules now show a stats bar at the top: live counts of Total · Debuff · Infinite · Tail-end
· Total = buffs that passed the gates; Debuff = is-debuff flag; Infinite = the infinite (permanent) flag; Tail-end = non-infinite buffs whose remaining/initial is below the countdown tail-end warning threshold.
· The bar takes a fixed height (1 point smaller than the name font); the grid shifts down and the module window grows by that height, so the text is never clipped.
== v23.13 ==
[1] The embedded default PNG for dodges 1-5 is replaced with a new image (the old "shrimp" icon is gone);
[2] Dodges 6/7 now have a "Style" dropdown: "Warning triangle" (the drawn red-bordered yellow triangle - old behaviour, still the default) or "Image";
[3] With "Image" you can use the built-in default warning image or point at any PNG (browse button included), plus a separate "Warning image scale" percentage;
[4] Flashing is now fully image-agnostic: the warning image reuses the exact same path as dodges 1-5 (fill pixels with alpha>128 in the flash color + a scale pulse), so any PNG just works;
[5] BUGFIX: the solid-flash cache was built once and never invalidated, so after changing the PNG or the flash color the flash frames still drew the old icon silhouette (the real cause of "flashing doesn't follow my new image"); the cache is now cleared every time the icons are reloaded;
[6] If the image is invalid or the path is empty it automatically falls back to the warning triangle - never a blank slot.
== v23.12 ==
[1] Spike 3D style dropdown cut to two options: 'Plain (no 3D effect)' and 'Diagonal two-tone (most like a 3D pyramid/diamond)';
[2] The 'Highlight Line' kept in V2311 is now fully removed; its parameter key spike_3d_line_width is deleted together (no dead code left);
[3] Restored the original V2264 two-tone algorithm — a hard-edge gradient (0.48 / 0.52 transitions) along the spike's left→right direction, with no light-direction involvement;
[4] Params spike_3d_twotone_light=130 / dark=110 (V2264 defaults); default spike_3d_style switched back to 'two_tone'.[1] 'two_tone' 3D style upgraded to 'Ridge-Face Aligned' — the ridge line always bisects the spike along its symmetry axis (root → tip), independent of light direction;
[2] which side is bright vs dark is decided by the sign of the light vector projected onto the axis-perpendicular;
[3] each face has its own light-direction linear gradient — vertices closer to the light are brighter, simulating physical illumination;
[4] new 'Gradient Amplitude' slider (0–100%) controls the per-face intensity, default 35%, degrades to flat color at 0;
[5] params spike_3d_twotone_light/dark (incl. dead-code twotone_angle) replaced by spike_3d_ridge_face_light/dark/amplitude; old settings.json files automatically pick up the new defaults.Debug-data mode (test UI without the game running) now has per-category injection counts: the "Debug Data" tab of both the All-Buff and Boss modules gets 4 count boxes - Normal Buff / Debuff / Permanent / Tail-end - each controlling how many fake buffs of that category to inject.
· Defaults to a mix (All-Buff: 8 normal + 2 debuff + 1 permanent + 1 tail-end; Boss: 5+1+1+1) so you see every possibility the moment debug is on; debug Debuff entries are now correctly classified as debuff for rendering (previously the unified override dict forced non-debuff, so they never showed). Changes apply live.
== v23.06 ==
Fixes the real cause of V2304 Debug Data showing blank All Buff / Boss modules while Core worked
· Root cause: debug fake buffs use sids 0xD001/D101 (53249/53505), far above the status_id_max you set for normal play (commonly 3000), so they were dropped entirely by the numeric gates; Core uses the active_buffs dict and skips that gate, hence it worked.
· Fix: in debug mode render_allbuff / render_bossbuff skip all gates and filter switches, so fake buffs always show regardless of any gate value; also removes the mistaken V2305 'snap to screen center' button (positions were already on-screen and unrelated to the blank issue).
== v23.05 ==
Fixes the V2304 Debug Data feature where modules were invisible
· Where: Settings → Global → General → Debug Data, new button at the top labeled 「📌 Snap 5 modules to screen center」.
· Default module positions (core y=568, All Buff y=1114, Boss y=8) often land under the taskbar or off-screen, so the All Buff and Boss fake data was being rendered in the background but not visible on the desktop.
· Click that button to instantly stack all five modules (Core / Dodge / Ability / All Buff / Boss) vertically, centered in the visible screen area - fake data is now right in front of you for live layout tuning.
== v23.04 ==
New Debug Data page: tune every module on your desktop without launching the game
· Location: Settings -> Global -> General -> Debug Data.
· Flip the master switch and the game no longer needs to be running: all five modules (Core, All Buffs, Boss Buffs, Ability Cooldown, Dodge) light up immediately with a full set of fake data so you can take your time tuning the look.
· Shared parameters: display text (editable, one string for everything), countdown value (8.88 by default), character status (OK / character not recognised / game not found / initialising), in-combat flag, mastery (none / Awakening / Truth / Secret).
· Core module: buff count 0-24 (3 by default), single-layer toggle, full-stack toggle (on by default), custom stack count when not full, max stacks (8 by default), Awakening / Truth / Secret flags.
· All-Buff module: card count 0-60 (12 by default), stacks, max stacks, infinite toggle.
· Boss Buff module: card count 0-60 (8 by default), stacks, max stacks, infinite toggle.
· Ability Cooldown / Dodge: ability slot count 0-4, how many slots are ready (1 by default), cooldown ceiling (30.0 s by default), dodge count 0-7 (7 by default).
· Every fake buff and skill name renders as the single string you typed, bypassing the buff-name and ability-name tables entirely, which makes it obvious at a glance whether long text will break your layout.
· Every option applies live - drag a value and the overlay updates instantly. Turning the master switch off returns to normal game-reading mode immediately.

== v23.03 ==
Smoother spike shading: gradient direction and strength now follow the shadow X/Y
- Hard edge kept: the bright and dark faces remain two colour blocks with a hard facet edge between them (that is what gives the prism/diamond look) - no smoothing is applied.
- Direction follows the light: the gradient axis is the shadow X/Y direction, so whichever side the light comes from is lit. When light arrives at an angle, the base and tip of each spike also pick up a brightness difference, which is far more three-dimensional than the old left/right split.
- Strength follows the offset: a larger shadow offset means stronger contrast; when X and Y are both 0 (or the shadow is disabled) it falls back to a flat colour.
- In short, both the direction and the magnitude of the shadow X/Y now control the gradient.

== v23.02 ==
Fixes another hidden bug on the 3D Effect tab: bright/dark sides swapped on most spikes
- Problem: the 12 spikes are radially arranged on a circle (one every 30°), but the Two-Tone and Edge Band styles have always used world-space coordinates for their gradient direction. That means only the top spike rendered correctly — every other spike had its bright and dark sides swapped.
- Fix: the body shading now rotates with each spike's local orientation, so the bright side is always the side facing the light.
- About the shadow: the shadow offset stays in world coordinates — a directional light is fixed in world space, so all shadows correctly fall in the same direction.

== v23.01 ==
The 3D Effect tab now applies changes live
- Problem: that page has 20 options, and 15 of them only took effect after closing the settings dialog — nothing changed on screen while dragging a slider or ticking a toggle, so you had to guess.
- Affected: shadow toggle and offset X/Y, shadow alpha, outline toggle and darkness, two-tone light/dark factors, edge band light/dark/width, bottom shadow darkness/height, and the bead light/dark factors.
- Fix: those 15 options now apply instantly, so you can tune while watching the effect.
- Note: this version only touches the 3D Effect tab; no other settings were changed.

== v23.00 ==
Fixed auto show/hide on game focus: not hiding on blur + only two modules returning
- Symptom 1: when the game went to the background, the two buff modules stayed visible (the other three hid correctly).
- Symptom 2: returning to the foreground, only those two buff modules came back; the core, dodge and skill modules stayed hidden.
- Cause: window visibility decisions were duplicated across four places that overrode each other. The focus timer (250 ms) had just hidden the windows when another code path, which only looked at the module toggle and knew nothing about foreground state, showed them again. It triggered whenever the in-combat or training-area state flipped while in the background, or a setting was changed.
- Because those two modules were always visible, the "any window visible" check was permanently true, so the "show all" action on returning to the foreground was skipped by its own condition and the other three never came back.
- Fix: all visibility decisions now go through a single arbiter with an explicit priority — game in background hides everything, then module toggles are applied. The four call sites now only record intent instead of touching windows directly.
- Bonus fix: the title bar minimize button used a raw hide loop that recorded no intent, so windows could reappear on their own after minimizing. It now uses the same arbiter.

== v22.64 ==
Fixes the v22.63 crash + adds a pre-build self-check script
- Problem: the new "3D Effect" settings sub-tab used QStackedWidget, but it was missing from the import list, so opening the settings dialog raised NameError and quit.
- Fix: added QStackedWidget to the PySide6.QtWidgets import list.
- New: a generic "undefined name" checker that scans the source for names used but never defined or imported, run before every build. Verified: removing the import makes it report the exact offending lines, so this class of mistake cannot slip through again.

== v22.63 ==
Spike/bead 3D shading is now a selectable, tunable style system: new "3D Effect" sub-tab under Core Detection.
- 5 spike styles: Solid / Midline Highlight (V2262) / Diagonal Two-Tone (most 3D, prism-diamond) / Wide Edge Band / Bottom Shadow
- 2 bead styles: Solid / Radial Sphere (recommended)
- Shared drop shadow: enable + offset X/Y + alpha (0-255)
- Shared dark outline: enable + width (0.0-3.0px) + darkness (100-200)
- Per-style parameters show/hide dynamically with the dropdown and apply live — compare and tune without rebuilding
- Defaults changed to Diagonal Two-Tone + shadow offset (4,5) + alpha 120
- 20 new settings, 39 new localized strings

== v22.62 ==
[Spike/bead depth rework: unified light + sphere shading + drop shadow]
1) Symptom: spikes previously used a wide root->tip linear gradient (dark 135% / light 140%), so your chosen spike color only appeared at 42% of the length - roots and tips were recolored, making it look "not solid, gradienty".
2) Fix: switched to a unified-light model (light from upper-left). Spikes are now filled with your exact solid color, plus only: ① a 1px ridge highlight along the axis (lighter 128) ② a 1px dark outline (darker 150) ③ a semi-transparent drop shadow offset to lower-right - color fidelity rises from ~60% to ~95% while keeping the 3D/thickness feel.
3) Decorative beads: replaced the flat darker(110) fill with a radial-gradient sphere (upper-left highlight lighter 160 -> base -> edge darker 125), giving an immediate spherical look.
4) Ring: added a semi-transparent dark ring shadow offset to lower-right, sharing the same light direction as spikes/beads for consistent "lifted" depth.
5) The white expansion flash when a new layer appears is unchanged - still a transient effect. No settings / UI / i18n were modified.
== v22.61 ==
【修正圓環外勾邊偏薄、且數值較小時不顯示的問題】
1) 現象：把「外勾邊」調到某個數值時，圓環上的勾邊明顯比尖刺和裝飾小球上的更細；而且數值調到 1 或 2 時，圓環上完全看不到勾邊，要調到 3 以上才出現。
2) 原因：圓環勾邊原本是貼著圓環中心線畫的，而圓環本體（帶陰影邊的那一層）會更晚繪製、直接蓋在上面。圓環本體比勾邊寬，於是把勾邊整個吃掉了一大半——數值小的時候全被蓋住，數值大了也只剩一小條。尖刺和裝飾小球是實心的，只會蓋住勾邊靠內的一半，所以看起來正常。
3) 修復：把圓環勾邊挪到圓環外沿再來畫，讓它露在外面的寬度與尖刺、裝飾小球完全一致。現在數值 1 起就能看到，且三者粗細一致。

== v22.60 ==
【新增門檻：剩餘時間 > 持續時間時丟棄】
1) 主控全 Buff 與 Boss Buff 兩個模組的「門檻」頁各新增一條**可勾選**規則：「⑤ 剩餘時間 > 持續時間丟棄」，預設開啟。
2) 作用：正常情況下剩餘時間不可能比持續時間還長。一旦出現這種情況，基本可以斷定讀到的是髒資料或異常殘留，勾選後直接丟棄該條，不顯示。
3) 例外：永續 buff（顯示為無窮符號）不受此限——它的持續時間記錄可能是 0，而剩餘時間殘留正數，這是正常現象，不會被誤殺。
4) 不需要時可在門檻頁取消勾選，該條檢查即被跳過。

== v22.59 ==
【修復「恢復預設」把設定沖掉的問題 + 按最新設定重新烘焙】
1) 修復「點恢復預設後，Buff 啟用/停用頁裡所有勾選框都被勾上」。原因不是烘焙出錯——烘焙進預設值的設定完全正確——而是重置程式碼裡有三處寫死的值繞過了預設值表：
   · Buff 專精勾選：重置時被無條件全部勾上（而不是按你設定裡實際的勾選狀態還原）。現已改為逐項還原。
   · 全域快捷鍵：三項被寫死成一組固定值，會把你配好的組合鍵沖掉，其中「鎖定視窗」「開啟設定」兩個還會被一併停用。現已改為按預設值還原。
   · 標題列對齊：寫死為「靠左」，你目前設定恰好也是靠左所以一直沒暴露。現已改為按預設值還原。
2) 已對「恢復預設」與「讀取設定」兩處程式碼做全量複查（合計 650 餘行），除以上三處外，再無其它繞過預設值表的寫死指派。
3) 同時用你**目前最新的設定**重新烘焙了一遍預設值（介面語言仍固定為簡體中文，不跟隨快照）。現在點「恢復預設」，結果應當與你最後一次開啟軟體時的狀態一致。

== v22.58 ==
【恢復預設的語言改回簡體中文】
1) 上一版把你的設定完整烘焙為預設值時，語言這一項被一併烘成了「日文」（因為烘焙時你正在使用日文介面）。
2) 已依照你的要求改回：點「恢復預設」時，介面語言統一回到**簡體中文**。
3) 除語言外的其餘全部設定保持不變，仍然是你烘焙進去的那一份設定。

== v22.57 ==
【把目前設定完整烘焙為預設值】
1) 應玩家要求，將你目前正在使用的這份設定（軟體目錄下的 overlay_settings.json，共 359 項）**完整烘焙進程式內建的預設值表**。今後點「恢復預設」，就會精確恢復成這份設定，而不再是一套出廠的通用參數。
2) 比對結果：內建預設值原本 351 項，你的設定有 359 項——你的設定完整覆蓋了內建預設值（沒有遺漏任何一項），並多出 8 項執行時期新增的設定。其中 **179 項的數值與舊預設值不同**，已逐項烘焙，沒有遺漏。
3) 順帶修復一處「恢復預設」的漏網問題：核心、翻滾、能力三個模組的縮放比例，原本在恢復預設時被寫死成 100%，不會跟隨預設值（例如你調過的核心模組 73% 會被沖掉）。現在改為與其它兩個模組一致，統一讀取預設值。

== v22.56 ==
【Buff 編號勘誤：混沌轉換】
1) 修正一處編號記錄錯誤：「混沌轉換」此前被登記為 編號 129（0x81），正確的編號應為 **148（0x94）**。判斷依據是軟體運行期自動記錄的未知 buff 清單——裡面實際出現的是 148（0x94），而 129（0x81）在遊戲裡從未出現過。
2) 已將內建名稱表中該條目的編號改正，四種語言的名稱（混沌轉換 / 混沌輪迴 / Chaos Shift / ケイオシフト）與屬性（單層、非角色專屬）全部原樣保留，條目總數不變。
3) 重要提示：軟體目錄下的「補充命名檔案」（buff_attrs_unknown.json）優先級高於內建名稱表。如果你之前的版本在運行目錄裡已經留下過 148（0x94）的佔位記錄，它會把新表裡的正確名字蓋掉——遇到「改了卻不生效」的情況，把該檔案刪除即可，重啟軟體後會自動重建，裡面仍然未知的編號不會遺失。

== v22.55 ==
【切換語言不生效——系統性修復】
1) 玩家回饋：切成日文後，設定面板裡「好多選項、好多文字仍然是中文」。逐項排查後結論是——資料層其實是乾淨的（介面翻譯表 566 條四語全非空、buff 名 119 條四語齊全、下拉選項 19 個裡 16 個在翻譯表內、另 3 個是語言名本就不該翻譯、原始碼零硬編碼中文），問題全部出在程式碼。
2) 主因：切語言時的文字刷新邏輯遍歷了標籤、核取方塊、按鈕、分組框四類控制項，卻「從不遍歷下拉選單的選項文字」——所以標籤正確變成日文了，而下拉選單裡的「按出現時間 / 居中 / 靠左 / 靠右 / 頂部對齊 / 底部對齊」仍然是中文。現已新增通用下拉選項翻譯：遍歷所有下拉選單，只改顯示文字、不動選項的綁定值，且僅當該文字在翻譯表內時才修改（角色名、buff 名等資料項會自動跳過，不會被誤傷）。
3) 三處手寫死的刷新程式碼會把日文/繁體強制寫回中文（例如「圓環」在日文下本應顯示「円環」，舊程式碼卻寫死成中文），已刪除，改由上面的通用邏輯統一處理。
4) 切換語言下拉選單時，此前只刷新設定視窗自身的文字，沒有把新語言推送給主介面。現已補上推送，切換後立即生效。
5) 五個模組視窗各自持有一份「啟動時拷貝」的設定快照，此前只有「設定面板即時拖動」這一條路徑會回寫它，而「設定視窗關閉」的兩條分支都不經過 → 模組內讀取語言的路徑（例如 buff 名稱）永遠停留在啟動時的舊值。現已在設定變化後統一回寫，修掉「必須重啟軟體才生效」的問題。

== v22.54 ==
[About page · 'Important memory addresses & data' panel i18n completed]
1) A player reported the panel did not switch languages. Investigation: the static offset reference table (21 lines) was **raw hardcoded strings** with no `_tr()` at all, so it always displayed Chinese regardless of the selected language; the live-section header and the mastery fallback 'unidentified' were also raw Chinese.
2) Fix: wrapped all 21 static lines in `_tr()` (the `"─"*64` separator is pure symbols, left untranslated); wrapped the live header and the three mastery names (Insight/Essence/Crux) plus the 'unidentified' fallback in `_tr()`.
3) Added 23 keys to i18n.json with zh_tw / en / ja (no zh — `_tr(zh)` returns the key itself as a fallback when lang=="zh", matching the 21 existing panel keys).
4) Technical data (hex offsets / version numbers / field names mgr, record, pptr, node_id) is **kept verbatim** in every language; only the Chinese descriptive parts are translated, so developers comparing offsets are not confused by translation.
5) Verified: the 21 pre-existing live-section keys (e.g. 'Module base    = ') already had all three translations and were not duplicated. i18n audit REAL MISSING=0.

== v22.53 ==
[Screen-edge alignment now applies at startup (fix: 'only works after opening Settings once')]
1) User report: the 'Screen horizontal/vertical alignment' options (left/right/center/top/bottom) only took effect after opening the Settings dialog once.
2) Root cause: `_refresh_window_geometries()` — the method that actually recomputes window coordinates from halign/valign — was only called in three places: (a) after confirming a window-position reset, (b) on screen resolution change (and only when res_scale actually changed), (c) after the Settings dialog closes via `_after_settings_changed()`. It was **never called at startup**, so on launch the modules used the raw X/Y from settings and the alignment was only applied once Settings had been opened.
3) Fix: call `self._refresh_window_geometries()` once at the end of controller init, right after all five module windows are `w.show()`n. Since the method itself runs `recalc_layout()` + `resize()` first, `w.width()/height()` are already final and `_screen_available_geometry()` is usable once QApplication exists — so a direct call works, no need to defer to the event loop.
4) Covers all five modules (core / roll / skill / all-buff / Boss); alignment is in place from the moment the app starts.

== v22.52 ==
[All modules · graded countdown decimals applied everywhere]
1) User asked to spread V2251's graded-decimal rule to every countdown in the app (skill module and core module included).
2) `_fmt_dur` was moved out of the "all-buff render" section to just before render_core, becoming a shared utility for all modules; its docstring now lists all 5 call sites so none get missed in future edits.
3) Three new call sites: skill module `_draw_skill_cd_element` `f"{cd_val:.2f}"` → `self._fmt_dur(cd_val)` (no 's' suffix for skill cooldown); core module render_core single-buff branch and fused/multi-buff branch `f"{timer_val:.2f}s"` → `f"{self._fmt_dur(timer_val)}s"` (keeps the 's' suffix).
4) The all-buff and Boss modules were already converted in V2251 and are unchanged. The roll module has no numeric countdown (nothing to change).
5) All 5 countdowns now share one rule: `|v| < 10` → 2 decimals (9.87); `10 <= |v| < 100` → 1 decimal (12.3); `|v| >= 100` → no decimals (123). V2215/V2216 infinity handling keeps its priority.

== v22.51 ==
[Both Buff modules · countdown decimals graded by integer-digit count]
1) User request: countdown <10 shows 2 decimals; a 2-digit integer part (10~99) shows only 1 decimal; an integer part longer than 2 digits (>=100) shows no decimals.
2) New render class method `_fmt_dur(self, v)`: `|v| < 10` → 2 decimals (e.g. 9.87); `10 <= |v| < 100` → 1 decimal (e.g. 12.3); `|v| >= 100` → no decimals (e.g. 123). Uses abs() to judge digit count, so negative dirty values also follow the rule instead of blowing up the card.
3) Both `time_str` sites (render_allbuff for the main module and render_bossbuff for the Boss module) now use `self._fmt_dur(rem)/self._fmt_dur(init)`; remaining and initial each get graded by their own magnitude.
4) The V2215/V2216 infinity logic (∞ / *∞ for FLT_MAX or >=9999) is unchanged and still takes priority.

== v22.46 ==
[Both Buff modules · buff-list rendering misalignment fully fixed]
1) Fixed a regression introduced in V2245 — V2245 kept maxHeight/minHeight on the wrapper container AND switched to Preferred policy while syncing inner.minHeight, which forced 9+ list rows (288px) into a 240px container, squashing every row from 32px to ~26px so the remove-button overlapped each row's text (user report: 'why is it even messier now').
2) V2246 fix — moved maxHeight/minHeight from the wrapper container down to the QScrollArea itself; the scroll area is still capped at 240px (to bound the dialog height) but the inner _inner now keeps its sizeHint (= N*32) under setWidgetResizable(True), and the scroll area auto-shows the vertical scrollbar when content exceeds the viewport.
3) Dropped the _container wrapper — the scroll area now lives straight in the form layout: one less layer of nesting, much more predictable behavior.
4) Changed the inner widget's vertical size policy from Preferred to MinimumExpanding — double insurance that the inner never shrinks below its minimum height.
5) Both modules (core all-buff + Boss Buff) and all four lists (bl/ml × 2) flow through the single _add_buff_list_group factory, so there is no longer a V2245-style 'fix one place, miss the other' risk.
6) Backfilled the v22.45 blocks that V2245 missed in release_notes.txt + README.md so the tri-lingual red-line is whole again.

== v22.44 ==
[Screen alignment · sync + manual-layout lock]
1) Sync fix: after changing "screen horizontal/vertical align", "horizontal/vertical margin", or "module scale" in Settings, the top "module position X/Y" now refreshes to the real on-screen coordinates immediately (fixes the previous lag where "layout changed but the top params didn't update together").
2) Manual-layout lock: on a non-"custom" align axis (left/right/center/top/bottom) manual layout is no longer allowed — that axis' X/Y box is greyed out and uneditable, and dragging the module or corner-resizing it with the mouse in-game is fully disabled (alignment IS parametric layout; manual layout would break it). Only "custom" re-enables mouse drag and resize.
All five modules (core / roll / skill / main all-Buff / Boss) get the symmetric change.

[Spike waist position · range unlocked + Gate page fully toggleable]
1) Spike waist position (spike_waist_pos): range widened from 5%~95% to 0%~100% — you can now push the spike ring all the way to the very top or bottom, with no dead margin at the canvas edge.
2) The 4 gate rules that were previously greyed-out / hard-disabled are now fully toggleable gating (main + Boss modules perfectly symmetric):
   · ① NaN/Inf check: can be turned OFF (when off, NaN/Inf junk values are also shown, for diagnostics).
   · ② ID=0/ID=1 cannot be infinite: now also filters "Defense UP (ID=1)" (previously only Attack UP ID=0); turning it off stops filtering them.
   · ③ Remaining/initial time ≤ X(seconds) drop: a new time input box added (default 0.0 s = no filter); players can set >0 to drop transient junk buffs.
   · ④ Duration cap (with 9999 infinite exemption): checkbox + number box (default 10000.0 s); unchecking disables the duration-cap filter.
The two hardened gates (hide 0.0/0.0, negative durations) remain always-on and cannot be turned off, as a safety net.
All toggles default ON; old configs auto-migrate with no manual change.

== v22.42 ==
[List widget · full rewrite (replaces the failed V2241 attempt)]
V2240 user screenshot reported the "Remove" button on every row of all 4 lists showed only "移"; V2241 tried setItemWidget + setSizePolicy(Expanding) to stretch it, but it still got clipped (user replied "still the same").
Root cause: QListWidget.setItemWidget renders the widget inside QListWidgetItem.sizeHint(); sizeHint defaults to the widget's preferred width, and setSizePolicy(Expanding) is **completely ignored** under setItemWidget — that is why V2241 failed.
Fix: replaced the list container of all 4 lists (main blacklist / main multi-instance / Boss blacklist / Boss multi-instance) from "QListWidget + setItemWidget" with "QScrollArea + inner QWidget + QVBoxLayout":
  (1) _scroll.setWidgetResizable(True) + inner _inner (sizePolicy Expanding) so _inner width auto-follows the _scroll viewport width;
  (2) row widget is added via _layout.insertWidget into the QVBoxLayout, taking _inner's full width (no item.sizeHint limit); label fills the left, the 76px "Remove" button shows fully on the right;
  (3) empty state now uses a centred dark-grey QLabel placeholder (replacing V2237's grey placeholder item) — cleaner logic;
  (4) row height uses setMinimumHeight(30) + QHBoxLayout natural height, no stretching/distortion.
All 4 lists take effect simultaneously. retranslate_ui path unchanged (button text still _tr("移除"); zh / zh_tw / en / ja automatic).

== v22.41 ==
[List widget · "Remove" button layout hotfix]
V2240 user screenshot reported "Remove" button on every row of all 4 lists (blacklist + multi-instance × main + Boss) showed only the character '移' with the second character clipped off.
Root cause: _refresh_buff_list uses QListWidget.setItemWidget to embed a custom row widget. Qt does NOT auto-stretch the widget to the item rect by default — row's default Preferred size policy → keeps sizeHint width → the label+button row gets compressed → setFixedWidth(64) effectively rendered only ~20-30px.
Fix:
  (1) Row widget now uses setSizePolicy(Expanding, Preferred) → forces it to fill the QListWidget item rect.
  (2) "Remove" button uses setMinimumWidth(76) + setMaximumWidth(76) (fixed-width with more headroom) + padding 2px6→3px8 + font-size 10→11 + min-height:22px + PointingHandCursor.
  (3) retranslate_ui path unchanged (button text still _tr("移除"); zh/zh_tw/en/ja automatic).

== v22.40 ==
[Core module · canvas width/height now adjustable]
Added two knobs "Canvas width (0=auto)" / "Canvas height (0=auto)" (Settings → Circle card, range 0–4000, 0 shows "auto"):
1) Default 0 = auto — keeps the old auto-computed layout exactly; existing users see zero change and need no reconfig.
2) A value >0 overrides the core module canvas (spike-circle + title bar + buff names). Content (circle / title / buff names) is already laid out relative to the canvas, so it auto re-centres both horizontally and vertically.

== v22.37 ==
• V2237 (22.37)：能力模組「聚散距離」下限解負——
  ① **UI SpinBox**：`聚散距離:` 下限由 20 放寬到 -200（與 `能力名X/Y偏移` / `倒計時X/Y偏移` 同款對稱風格），範圍（-200, 200），可輸入負值讓 4 個技能菱形向畫布中心靠攏甚至完全重疊。

== v22.36 ==
• V2236 (22.36): cleanup + 3 fixes——
  1) **Removed the "All-Buff submodule flash" feature**: added in V2107, it flashed the submodule. User reported "if I don't tick this flash option, the main All-Buff module just vanishes" — the root cause was a stale variable throwing in a path whose exception was swallowed by paintEvent's try/except, blanking the whole module. Keeping it only confused players and could flicker, so it is fully removed: the flash_apply_allbuff_submodule / boss_flash_apply_allbuff_submodule keys are dropped from DEFAULT_SETTINGS, the 3 corresponding checkboxes in the settings panel are removed, and the flash animation timer/fields are gone.
  2) **Rebuilt all 4 list UIs**: Main blacklist / Main multi-occurrence / Boss blacklist / Boss multi-occurrence. Changed from "QScrollArea + vertical stretch (evenly distributed)" to "small frame (QGroupBox) wrapping a QListWidget + per-row setItemWidget + Native scrollbar". The inner list uses natural row heights and scrolls when overflowing; it no longer blows up the GroupBox and rows never overlap. All 4 lists updated together.
  3) **All gates now exposed**: the upper part of the "Gates" sub-tab for both Main and Boss gets 6 disabled "baked-in gates (always on, cannot disable)" checkboxes that explicitly list every baked-in gate — NaN/Inf check / status_id=0 can't be infinite / hide 0.0/0.0 countdown / drop if remaining or initial < 0 / **⑤ an infinite buff's remaining can't be <=0 [NEW in V2236]** / duration cap 10000s (with 9999 infinite exemption). The adjustable numeric gates below (status_id / sub_id cap / stack cap / min remaining / min initial / min appearance duration) are kept. Two hard-coded checks added at the render side: `if sid_i==0 and infinite: continue` and `if infinite and remaining<=0.0 and remaining<9999.0: continue`.
  4) **Card centering review**: confirmed the progress bar and text in both buff-module submodules (drawText / addText / bar_x / by formulas) are strictly horizontally + vertically centered, and added a V2236 centering-review comment.

== v22.35 ==
• V2235 (22.35): final fix for the low-level rendering bug behind "Main All-Buff blank" - V2234's auto-clamp didn't solve it, so the root cause is in the rendering layer rather than the clip. V2235 ships three hard changes:
  1) **Render sanity bands**: at the very start of _draw_allbuff_card (right after painter.save), draw three glaring horizontal bars (red 255,0,0 / blue 0,128,255 / green 0,220,90) covering the top 1~14px of the card.
     - If you can see the bands -> the render pipeline reaches the card layer; the issue is in backing_col / name / stacks / bar colors.
     - If you cannot see the bands -> painter state, outer coordinate system, or widget clipping is broken (not in this function).
     This is the spirit of the user's quote "spend more time on the lower-level logic errors": for any future draw-pipeline issue, this is the first thing to wire up.
  2) **Dump atomicity**: previously, the dict literal at line 8790 - if any single field's evaluation raised, the whole _dump_allbuff_buffs call was swallowed by the outer except, leaving the dump file with whatever the LAST successful run had written. That is exactly why V2234's dump came out without win_geo / layout / cards / alpha and I mistakenly concluded V2234 had not been run.
     Now each field is wrapped in its own try/except + lambda, with any field-level error downgrading to err:ExceptionType. The whole dump call cannot fail because of one bad field; the next run will surface the full diagnostic fields immediately.
  3) **V2234's auto-clamp is kept as-is**: it keeps the backdrop / border visible by clamping the whole module onto the screen, but a low-level rendering error can still produce a blank card even with the clip gone.

== v22.34 ==
• V2234 (22.34): After V2233's self-healing went live, the real root cause of "Main All-Buff blank" turned out NOT to be a stale pptr but a physical off-screen clip:/n  1) Positions are saved normalized to a 1920-wide canvas; res_scale = max(1.0, screen_w/1920). On a 2560x1440 monitor that is 1.333, so a stored y of 1030 becomes 1373 - seemingly 67px clear of the screen bottom (1440);
  2) With valign=bottom + vmargin=-40, the y is pushed another 40px to 1413, leaving only 27px of room;
  3) The canvas height is bh(=56) * disp_h(=1.333) ~= 75px, but the Qt window has already been clipped 16px by the screen bottom, so the actual window_h is only 59px;
  4) The card body (name / stacks / time / bar) sits in the canvas 30~60px band - exactly inside the 16~43px clip range, so the user only ever sees the backdrop / border, never the buff name or timer. This matches the reported "Main empty, Boss fine" symptom perfectly.
  Fix: a new auto-clamp at the end of _refresh_window_geometries: when the window's bottom edge goes past the available area, the y is pulled up to max(screen_top, screen_bottom - window_h) so the whole window fits on screen. Horizontal overflow (left/right) is handled the same way but with a lower priority than explicit placement - if the user has deliberately dragged a window off-screen, the auto-clamp leaves it alone. V2233's two dumps (_dump_allbuff_buffs render-level / _dump_allbuff_source data-source-level) and the pptr self-healing logic (_pptr_broken flag + 3s AOB-rescan throttle) are kept in place.

== v22.33 ==
• V2233 (22.33): Fixes "Boss module shows values but the Main All-Buff module is always empty". The two modules read from asymmetric sources: Boss uses read_boss_buffs(module_base), where module_base comes straight from get_module_info and is ALWAYS correct; Main uses read_overlay_data(self.pptr), where pptr comes from the ptr cache / AOB reverse-resolve and CAN go stale (a stale cache is trusted whenever it happens to deref to any non-zero garbage value; AOB can also mis-hit) -> char_base becomes 0 or a garbage address -> read_overlay_data returns no_char with an empty all_buffs_list -> the Main All-Buff module stays blank forever while Boss keeps working.
  - Self-healing: when status == no_char, OR status == ok but BOTH charid_hash and char_type are 0 (meaning char_base is a garbage address), the ptr cache is deleted and pptr is re-resolved via AOB; a fresh snapshot is then read immediately with the new pointer, restoring the Main module (throttled to once every 3s so the 80MB full-module scan can't tank the frame rate).
  - Diagnostic bypass: new _dump_allbuff_buffs writes, every second, "post-filter items + pre-filter raw + pointer-chain context (pptr / char_base / charid_hash / char_type / pl_id / boss_actor / boss_buff_count)" to EXE_DIR/last_allbuff_buffs.json - fully symmetric with the existing last_boss_buffs.json - to tell apart three root causes: empty data source / everything dropped by gates / render never executed.
== v22.32 ==
• V2232 (22.32): Corrects V2231's misplacement - the 3 boss buffs (sid=149/146/129) are moved OUT of the core detection module's per-character profile table (i18n.json buffs) and INTO the global buff name table (buff_attrs.json), which is what the Boss module and All-Buff module actually read for names (via _attr_for_sid -> BUFF_ATTRS). V2231 wrongly injected them into every character's bucket - polluting the character profiles AND not fixing boss display at all (boss names don't come from i18n.json buffs).
  - sid=149 (0x95) 世界裂痕 / Fractured World / 龜裂世界 / ワールドクラック
  - sid=146 (0x92) 纯白之境 / Proto-White World / 白堊境界 / 白亜の境界
  - sid=129 (0x81) 混沌转换 / Chaos Shift / 混沌輪迴 / ケイオシフト
  - Attributes: single-layer, non-exclusive, non-debuff. Boss fights now show proper 4-language names for these sids instead of falling back to 0x0095/0x0092/0x0081. i18n.json character buffs reverted to 119 entries (87 wrongly-added removed); buff_attrs.json gained 3 entries (144->147). No source-code logic change.

== v22.29 ==
• V2229 (22.29): Promoted "Screen H/V Alignment" + "H/V Margin (can be negative)" to all 5 modules (Core / Dodge / Skill / Main AllBuff / Boss).
  - New "Horizontal/Vertical Margin" spinbox (-2000..2000 px) applies to Top/Bottom/Left/Right alignment. Positive pushes outward, negative pulls inward (may overlap taskbar / overflow off-screen).
  - To align flush with the physical screen bottom (overlap the taskbar), set "Vertical Margin" to a negative value (≈ -taskbar height). Negative values are fully supported.
  - Defaults are "Custom / 0" = use the X/Y coordinates directly, identical to old behavior — zero impact for existing users.
  - Recalculated on every window refresh (scaling / resolution change), always kept aligned.
  • Bug: the "bottom alignment floats up a bit" issue — root cause is availableGeometry() excluding the taskbar; the new margin option lets you compensate with a single negative value.

[V2228/22.28] - Added a "Screen Alignment" setting to both the "Main all-Buff module" and the "Boss Buff module" (under the Position & Scale subtab): horizontal = Custom/Left/Center/Right, vertical = Custom/Top/Center/Bottom, both default to "Custom" (uses the original X/Y coordinates, identical to the old behavior). When not Custom, every window refresh (module scaling, resolution change, per-row / row-count change) recomputes that axis against the primary screen's available area (taskbar excluded) — horizontal: Left snaps to the left edge, Center centers horizontally, Right snaps to the right edge; vertical: Top snaps to the top edge, Center centers vertically, Bottom snaps to the bottom edge. The two modules are fully independent. The V2227 list-scrollbar fix is unaffected.
== v22.20 ==
== v22.21 ==
[V2227/22.27] - Fixed the real root cause of "list rows collapsing into an unreadable pile when there are many entries". Until now it looked like a simple "not enough height, last row gets clipped" problem (V2221 added the scroll area, V2225 raised max height 180→220), but the container was actually being **squashed by the QScrollArea**: the scroll area uses setWidgetResizable(True) while the inner container had no sizePolicy, so it was force-compressed to the viewport height — N rows got crammed into insufficient space, row heights squeezed, and the buff name overlapped the "移除" (Remove) button (player screenshot: the 7th entry of the Boss blacklist still overlapped). Fix: give the inner container a sizePolicy of (Preferred, Maximum) so it keeps its natural content height — the scrollbar now **actually appears** once content exceeds the max height, and content is never compressed. Max height also relaxed to 260px (~7-8 rows visible by default). Lists now never overlap no matter how many entries you add; they simply scroll. Applies to all 4 lists (main blacklist, main multi-instance list, Boss blacklist, Boss multi-instance list).
[V2226/22.26] - Two changes: 1) The Boss Buff module now has a hard-coded internal gate (always on, no toggle) — a boss can never have "character-exclusive" or "mastery" buffs (those belong only to the player's character), so the renderer now drops them unconditionally. The old boss_exclude_exclusive / boss_exclude_mastery keys are kept only for backwards compatibility with existing configs and no longer affect the decision. **The same switches in the main All-Buff module are unchanged**, since your own character really can have exclusive/mastery buffs. 2) Both the main All-Buff module and the Boss Buff module get a new "Row Alignment" setting (Left / Center / Right, **default Left**): it controls how buff cards are laid out horizontally within each row — "Left" starts at the row start (same as older versions), "Center" centers the cards of a partially filled row, "Right" pushes them to the row end. All three are identical for full rows; the difference only shows on the last row when it holds fewer cards than "Cards per row". Found in each module's "Layout" sub-tab.
[V2225/22.25] - Fixed a bug where the 6th row of the buff-name lists rendered as garbled overlapping text. The QScrollArea added in V2221 had a fixed max height of 180px, which landed exactly on the '6 rows' boundary: the bottom of the 6th row got clipped by the scroll area, so the QLabel text (buff name) overlapped the '移除' (Remove) button on the right and turned into unreadable mojibake (reported via screenshot on the 6th entry of the Boss blacklist). Max height is now relaxed to 220px (~7-8 rows), so 6 rows display fully and scrolling only kicks in at 7-8 rows. Both list containers (main / Boss) are changed, so all 4 lists (main blacklist, main multi-instance list, Boss blacklist, Boss multi-instance list) are fixed at once.
[V2224/22.24] - The external supplement file buff_attrs_unknown.json is now a player-editable override with live hot-reload: unknown buffs are no longer just a diagnostic log — players can fill in 名称/繁中名/英文名/日文名 (zh/zh-tw/en/ja) directly, and changes apply on the next frame (<1s) after saving, no restart or reinstall needed. Unknown buff attrs now include a 日文名 placeholder (fixing the V2223 regression where unknown buff names were blank in Japanese). Both the main AllBuff module and the Boss Buff module hook into the hot-reload. The external rename channel removed in V2220 returns in a more complete form — live hot-reload instead of loading only once at startup.
[V2223/22.23] - Fixed buff names showing Simplified Chinese under Japanese language mode. The i18n.json buffs section already had JA translations, but the name mapping in the live overlay / All-Buff module / settings-panel lists all missed 'ja': 1) read_overlay_data did not write the "ja" field when assembling buff entries, so _buff_name(buff,"ja") always fell back to Simplified Chinese; 2) the All-Buff card name_key mapping and the settings-panel list fallback mapping missed ja->Japanese. Both fixed; buff names now display correctly in Japanese.
[V2222/22.22] - Restored status_id/sub_id upper-limit GATE (threshold) + sid==0 always shows. ① Removed the V2217 hardcoded `if sid_i == 0: continue` and `if sid_i == 0 and infinite: continue` — sid==0 buffs (e.g. DMG UP) now display. ② status_id/sub_id upper limits changed from hardcoded 0xFFFF constant back to a configurable gate: both the 'All Buff' and 'Boss Buff' 'Gate' pages regain a 'status_id max' and 'sub_id max' checkbox+spinbox, default 0xFFFF (same as old hardcoded; only a player-lowered threshold filters). One change touches render gate + settings keys + UI controls.
[V2221/22.21] - Add QScrollArea scrollbar to all buff lists — both the 'All Buff' and 'Boss Buff' modules each have a 'Blacklist' and a 'Multi-instance' pair (4 lists total). Previously these stacked added-buff rows directly into a plain QVBoxLayout with no max height and no scrollbar, so a long list would be cropped by the dialog. Now all 4 lists wrap the inner QVBoxLayout in a QScrollArea with a fixed 180px max height (~6–7 rows), the dark-themed scrollbar matches the UI, and a single change inside the shared factory _add_buff_list_group covers all 4 lists. Title bar shows V2221.
[English]
V2220: Removed the external player dictionary buff_attrs_user.json. Data source is now built-in buff_attrs.json (bundled in the exe) plus the single external diagnostic file buff_attrs_unknown.json; the only buff-related json next to the exe is buff_attrs_unknown.json. Unknown buffs always show their hex ID with no external renaming channel; buff_attrs_unknown.json is diagnostic-only. If you identify what an ID is, please report it to the author with the ID.

Added 15 missing four-language keys; diagnostic f-strings rewritten as 'translated static fragment + dynamic value'. == v22.18 ==

- New "Lock windows on startup" toggle (Settings → Global → General). When enabled the app starts locked, preventing accidental drag/resize; unlock via the title-bar lock icon, global hotkey (Lock/Unlock), or tray menu.

== v22.17 ==
[V2217/22.17] - Fixed the height bug where multi-row buffs got sliced off by the module window. Root cause: the card draw height (render side) still used the old 4-row formula (name / stacks / time / bar), while the per-frame fixed window sizing (_apply_allbuff_fixed_size / _apply_boss_fixed_size) and the actual card content (_draw_*_card) had already been fused into 3 rows (name / stacks / time+bar) back in V2209. The 4-row formula made each card ~13px taller than the window slot, so from the 2nd row down the card bottoms overflowed the window and got clipped. This version aligns the render-side height formula and both modules' recalc_layout to the exact same fused-3-row + QFontMetrics formula used by the fixed-size function, so draw / fixed-size / recalc-layout all agree and multi-row buffs display fully.
- Removed the ∞ perpetual symbol from the right of buff names. The remaining / duration bar already shows ∞ (true-perpetual -> plain `∞`; value-too-large auto-detected -> `*∞`), so the name no longer needs it -- this also stops long buff names from being squeezed by the ∞ into staircase-shrunk text or an ellipsis. AllBuff (main) and Boss modules both updated.
- The four threshold UI controls (status_id cap / sub_id cap / stack-conflict check / max-duration) are now hardcoded to always-on (no toggle; render side enforces the filter unconditionally); the progress-bar and backing width / height have been moved into each module's Layout tab (both AllBuff and Boss). These two are finalized together in this version.

== v22.16 ==
[V2216/22.16] - **Max-duration gate overhaul**: when remaining/initial is ≥ 9999 s, the buff is now treated as perpetual (bypasses the durmax drop) — broader than V2214's `>= 1e30 (FLT_MAX)` fallback. V2214 only handled the float32-max sentinel; V2216 also keeps real long buffs in the 9999–100000 s range (e.g. some transformation / long-buff forms).
- **Time display distinguishes 'true' vs 'auto-detected' perpetual**: `infinite=True` (game-flagged) → plain `∞`; `infinite=False` but value ≥ 9999 (auto-detected) → `*∞` (asterisk prefix signals this is our own call, not a game-declared perpetual). Both AllBuff and Boss modules in sync.
- **Removed the two `Filter status_id == 0 (DMG UP, etc.)` checkboxes** (one in AllBuff, one in Boss): the filter is now hard-coded as 'always on' — the render side unconditionally does `if sid_i == 0: continue`, so DMG-UP-class buffs (sid=0) never show, and no UI toggle is needed. The two settings keys `allbuff_gate_filter_status_id_zero` / `boss_gate_filter_status_id_zero` are removed from DEFAULT_SETTINGS; a load-time migration also pops them from the user's on-disk config. i18n.json drops the matching UI string (no residue in any of the 4 languages).

== v22.15 ==
[V2215/22.15] - **Companion display fix for V2214**: FLT_MAX (~3.4e38) means a permanent/untimed buff in the game. V2214 stopped the duration-max gate from dropping those buffs, but the card's time row still printed `f"{rem:.1f}/{init:.1f}"`, producing an astronomical number like `340282346638528860000000000000000000000.0/...` that blew the card layout apart. Now, when `remaining` or `initial` is >= 1e30, the time row shows `∞` instead. Applied to both the Master All-Buff and Boss modules.

== v22.14 ==
[V2214/22.14] - **Fixed: permanent/untimed buffs were never displayed.** The game represents a permanent buff as float32 max (FLT_MAX ~ 3.4e38) in remaining/initial, but such a buff's `infinite` flag is **not necessarily True** - measured sid=0x92(146) has `infinite=False` with `remaining = initial = 3.4028234663852886e+38`. The duration-max gate (default 10000 s) treated FLT_MAX as "garbage exceeding the duration cap" and dropped those buffs, so permanent buffs never appeared in the Boss / All-Buff modules. Fix: treat a huge remaining/initial (>= 1e30) as permanent and exempt it from the duration-max gate. Applied to both the Master All-Buff and Boss loops.
- **Companion debug facility (V2212/V2213)**: `render_bossbuff` now dumps both the pre-filter raw data and the post-filter items to `EXE_DIR/last_boss_buffs.json` every second. Diffing the two shows **exactly which buff was dropped by which gate** - no need to look at the game window (when paused, the pause menu covers the screen so screenshots can't capture the overlay itself). Pure read-only side channel, display logic unaffected.

== v22.12 ==
[V2212/22.12] - V2212 debug: throttled dump of render_bossbuff filtered items to EXE_DIR/last_boss_buffs.json (every 1 second). Purpose: when the game is paused, the pause menu covers the game window, so ImageGrab.grab can only capture the menu - not the boss overlay itself. With this dump, players (or automation) can directly read last_boss_buffs.json to get the boss's current buffs (sid, name, remaining, initial, unknown flag) without any screenshot. Pure read-only side channel, **does not affect display logic or any gate behavior**.

== v22.11 ==
[V2211/22.11] - The three gates V2117 locked in (NaN/Inf check / sid=0-not-infinite / 0.0/0.0 countdown discarded) gained a **fourth**: if either **remaining < 0 or initial < 0**, the buff is dropped. Reason: occasionally a buff shows "-0.5/8.5" during state transitions or due to data glitches - the old 0.0/0.0 gate couldn't catch negative values. Now both are merged into a single check: `if _zr < 0 or _zi < 0 or f"{_zr:.1f}/{_zi:.1f}" == "0.0/0.0": continue`. Applied symmetrically to the Master All-Buff and Boss loops (two edits). Also updated every in-source "V2117 三门限已固化" wording to "V2117+V2211 四门限已固化".

== v22.10 ==
[V2210/22.10] - Removed the Boss module's "分类显示 / Category" sub-tab (the five exclude switches: already-shown-in-core, infinite, character-exclusive, mastery-exclusive and single-layer). The setting keys and the internal render-side checks are kept, so old configs and hand-edited json still work.
- Fixed "the same buff shows up twice" (e.g. Burn A rendered as two identical cards). Root cause: ExStatus is a pointer array, and the same status object can be pointed to by several slots - the old code read it once per slot, collecting the same buff multiple times. Since players had added those sids (Burn, Poison, ...) to the multi-instance list - intending it for genuinely stacked layers like Burn A + Burn B - the duplicate reads got displayed too. Now deduplicated in two stages, **without harming real multi-instances**: (1) at read time, dedupe by status pointer address (the same address is read once); (2) at render time, dedupe by content fingerprint (same sid AND identical sub_id / stacks / remaining / initial / infinite). Genuinely different instances such as Burn A (8.5s left) + Burn B (3.2s left) are still all shown. The Master All-Buff and Boss modules share the same reader, so both are fixed.

== v22.09 ==
[V2209/22.09] - Fixed: the Master All-Buff module's "Module Scale" was hijacked by the Boss module's scale (Boss scale controlled both; Master scale did nothing). Root cause: the Boss scale row was built with key "allbuff", so _mk_scale_row's internal setattr overwrote the Master's widget references.
- Fused countdown text into the progress bar: the countdown text now sits centered on top of the bar (same row, dark outline keeps it readable over the bar). Cards shrink from 4 rows (name / stacks / time / bar) to 3, saving a full row of vertical space. Applies to both the Master All-Buff and Boss Buff modules.
- Buff attribute dictionary is now external: on first run the tool writes buff_attrs_user.json next to the exe (all 144 entries). Edit names and attributes freely; the external JSON wins and merges per field, so changing only a name keeps 是否专属 / 单层 and the rest.
- New "特殊独立" (special / standalone) attribute: marks buffs that do not come from ExStatus and need their own offset and read path - 5 in total: Gran / Djeeta's Adept Arts Level, Vaseraga's Grynoth Gauge Hold, Id's Hidden Gauge, Fraux's Enchantress's Blessing. Their sids are negative placeholders that collide with each other, so they are listed in the external dictionary under SP:{PL}:{idx} keys; all are inherently character-exclusive.
- The Master All-Buff module now also supports unknown buffs: like the Boss module in V2208, uncataloged buffs are no longer dropped but show their hex ID. Only unknown buffs that pass every gate get recorded, dumped to buff_attrs_unknown.json next to the exe every 5 seconds - fill in the name there and copy the entry into buff_attrs_user.json to make it permanent.

== v22.08 ==
[V2208/22.08] Boss Buff module no longer silently drops buffs missing from buff_attrs.json — previously render_bossbuff did `continue` when `BUFF_ATTRS.get(...)` returned None, so any buff not yet cataloged (new DLC / DB gap / player-reported miss) was never rendered at all and players assumed the tool didn't see it. Now: unknown buffs synthesize a minimal attr (名称 / 繁中名 / 英文名 all become "0x{sid:04X}"), so the player sees the raw ID in the Boss module and can (a) add it to buff_attrs.json later; (b) copy the ID straight into an issue report. Filter switches (boss_exclude_*, boss_gate_*) / blacklist / multi-instance whitelist are unchanged — manual suppression still works. No new i18n.json keys (names are dynamically hex). Title-bar reports V2208.

== v22.07 ==
[V2207/22.07] Fixed the triple bug where the Boss Buff module's values could not be edited at all and changes never reflected to the live UI: ① get_settings() only wrote back 3 boss keys (boss_window_x / boss_window_y / boss_scale_percent); all other boss_* keys (layout / category / gate / font / backing / warning / canvas) were never written back to settings — editing controls did nothing. ② The Boss gate's 8 pairs of widgets mistakenly reused self.gate_* (same names as the Master All-Buff module's self.gate_*); later construction overwrote earlier, so AllBuff's self.gate_* references were silently swapped to Boss widgets (which also broke AllBuff gate save/reset), and the Boss section had no self.boss_gate_* attribute to reference. ③ Missing signal connections: boss_exclude_* (5), boss_gate_* (filter_status_id_zero + check_stack_conflict + 8 chk/spn pairs), and boss_canvas_bg_opacity_spn were never connected to _emit_changed. Fix: renamed all 16 Boss gate attributes to self.boss_gate_* (eliminating the name collision with AllBuff and incidentally fixing AllBuff gate save/reset too); connected all the missing Boss signals; completed all non-color boss_* keys in get_settings / reset_defaults (color keys are already covered by the generic color/opacity loops, no extra work). == v22.00 ==
[V2200/22.00] New "Boss Buff" module (5th module): a separate overlay window that shows every buff/debuff currently on the boss in real time (ATK DOWN, DEF DOWN, Poison, Burn, Slow, etc.). Its data source is fully decoupled from your own character: the boss body is located through the game's entity table (entity name containing "enemy", or uppercase Em + 4 digits, e.g. placement_enemy_member / Em7202), then its ExStatus is read (component at actor+0xCF8, list begin pointer at actor+0xD10). Verified in-game to reliably read every status id / stack count / remaining time on the boss. The module follows the same three shared traits as the existing modules: independent show/hide switch (Settings -> Module switches), independent on-screen X/Y position, and independent overall scale, plus its own "Boss Buff" settings tab. Its appearance (colors / layout / thresholds / lists) reuses the Master All-Buff module's settings, so both stay in sync - tune once, both match. When not in combat or the boss has no status, only the empty backdrop is drawn (no placeholder text). Boss lookup is cached for 1.5 s so the main loop is unaffected. Title bar reports V2200.

== v21.17 ==
[V2117/21.17] Three gates are now permanent (always on, no longer expose any switch): ① NaN/Inf check (data correctness — players should not be able to disable it); ② ID=0 exclude-infinite (Attack UP can never be infinite; garbage entries set the infinite flag); ③ Hide countdowns showing 0.0/0.0 (infinite buffs have meaningless time fields). UI removes the three QCheckBoxes plus their signal connections / reset / save; render side drops the g_nan / g_zero_notinf / g_hide_zero local variables and the corresponding `if` guards (logic now runs unconditionally); settings save still writes True for backward config compatibility; DEFAULT_SETTINGS keeps the three keys with a comment marking them as permanent. The remaining numeric gates (status_id max / sub_id max / stacks max / stack-conflict / duration max / min remaining / min initial / min appearance) stay user-tunable. Title bar reports V2117.

== v21.16 ==
[V2116/21.16] Top-level tab "All-Buff" renamed to "Master All-Buff"; the "Filter" sub-tab renamed to "Categorization" (semantically more accurate: it only governs display-layer switches). The old "Multi-Appearance Whitelist" section is rebuilt into two QGroupBoxes under a new "Lists" sub-tab: Blacklist (placed first; listed sids never show in the All-Buff module, useful for suppressing transient shields that linger for one silent frame) + Multi-Appearance List (placed second; listed sids are exempt from the single-instance cap so multiple instances can stack). Both GroupBoxes share the same structure and UI; the dropdown uses three-layer filtering (character-exclusive buffs hidden + already-on-this-list hidden + already-on-the-other-list hidden). The render side now reads both lists (multi_buff_blacklist / multi_buff_whitelist); blacklist sids are filtered out first, before any other gate / switch. New settings["multi_buff_blacklist"] default []. Title bar reports V2116. Note: character-exclusive buffs that slipped into either list during V2110-V2115 are now hard-filtered out at render time (double safety: UI also hides them).

The added-list also hides any exclusive sid accidentally present (legacy V2110 config defense); the render-side hard guard is kept. Title bar reports V2113.

== v21.10 ==
[V2110/21.10] All-Buff module restores "default one instance per buff" (reverting V2108/V2109 per-slot multi-instance behavior): de-duplicate by sid, keeping only the first instance per same-name/same-sid and skipping the rest, matching the in-game UI. Added a "Multi-Instance Whitelist" — players can add buffs allowed to appear multiple times in the settings panel (All-Buff module -> Gates -> Multi-Instance Whitelist) by id (decimal or 0x hex); sids on the whitelist bypass de-duplication and can show multiple instances. == v21.07 ==
[V2107/21.07] Three things: (1) QSS now fully hides QSpinBox native up/down buttons and QComboBox native dropdown arrow. V2091's width:0px did nothing for vertically-stacked spinbox buttons, leaving ▲▼ next to every number field (useless on click) and an isolated ▽ to the right of every combo box on Windows. Now uses height:0px plus image:none + border:none to truly zero both sub-controls, and the same treatment for QComboBox::drop-down / ::down-arrow (so every QComboBox native arrow vanishes — V2080 already used an independent QLabel to own the right-side look of the Sort Mode combo; this clears the rest of the dialog). (2) New "All-buff submodule flash" toggle in the Flash Apply To card (on by default), listed alongside Spike / Skill ready / Dodge icon flash. render_allbuff now does an appear/disappear diff per sub-module: a sid in last frame's placed set but missing this frame → disappear flash; a sid newly added → appear flash. Flash shape = card-rect outline (1.5 px in flash_color) + translucent fill (fill alpha 60). Appear pulse curve = 0.3 ramp-up / 0.7 ramp-down (matching spike flash); disappear fades alpha 1→0 directly. Reuses the global flash_color / flash_duration_ms. (3) i18n schema bumped 89 → 95, plus the new "All-buff submodule flash" trilingual key. Title bar reports V2107.

== v21.05 ==
- Debuff classification is now table-driven instead of a numeric threshold. Debuffs are decided by the new `是否debuff` (is-debuff) field in buff_attrs.json (merged from the last column of buff_attrs_v4.xlsx), replacing the old `sid ≥ 1000` hard cutoff. Known buffs use the table flag (the 26 known debuffs are correctly flagged, including Poison/Burn/Slow/Dizzy/Glaciate ailments and low-id debuffs like ATK↓/DEF↓/Max HP↓; notably 1009/1010/1013/1014 are ≥1000 but NOT flagged, so they correctly show as Buffs). Unknown ids fall back to the old `sid >= 1000` rule as a safety net. Also removed the "ailment classification threshold" gate setting (and its UI/connect/reset/save wiring). Version +1 (V2104 → V2105, title bar reports 21.05), schema unchanged.

== v21.04 ==
- All-Buff module logic simplified: (1) Debuffs no longer get their own row — all Buffs come first, then all Debuffs appended after them in their existing sort order (no row break); (2) "Per Row" (per_row) and the new "Rows" (allbuff_rows) are both adjustable again; the module's overall size = per_row × rows × card size + row/column gaps, derived dynamically from these two values and the card size/gaps (it no longer scales with buff count, and a fixed grid is reserved even when empty / no character); (3) capacity = per_row × rows, overflow truncated; (4) removed the "Debuff Force New Row" toggle and its setting/i18n/reset/save wiring. Version +1 (V2103 → V2104, title bar reports 21.04), schema unchanged.

== v20.68 ==
- Fixed the deeper "NaN-to-zero" root cause that V2067 left in: read_exstatus_buffs was writing NaN/Inf duration fields as 0 (GBFR stores NaN for infinite / pending buffs). Those buffs entered all_buffs_filtered and were then killed by the render gate's `min_initial_time < 0.05s` and `min_remaining_time < 0.05s` checks — leaving the entire All-Buff module blank (the Core module is unaffected, as it does not read initial/remaining).
- Now matches GBFR_BuffMonitor's _parse_statusbase: the reader discards any buff with NaN/Inf duration at parse time (continue skip). Render also adds a `not infinite` guard to `min_initial_time` for defense.
- All V2067 5+5 numeric gates / BUFF_ATTRS whitelist / 5 legacy filter toggles are unchanged — the Indicator and the Monitor are now behaviorally identical with matching gate settings.

== v20.67 ==
- Fixed a bug introduced in v20.66 in the Gate's stack-conflict check: the `cur_stacks>max_stacks` test lacked the `max_stacks>0` guard. GBFR reports max_stacks==0 ("uncapped/undefined") for many buffs, so every buff with any stacks was wrongly treated as a "stack conflict" and dropped — leaving the entire All-Buff module blank. Now matches GBFR_BuffMonitor: conflict is only judged when max_stacks>0.

== v20.66 ==
- Reverted the V2064/V2065 blacklist/whitelist "Junk Filter" and replaced it with the Gate (from GBFR_BuffMonitor) numerical junk filter. Each threshold is independently toggleable and live: filter status_id==0 / status_id max / sub_id max / ailment threshold / current-stacks max / max-stacks max / stack conflict / duration max / min remaining / min initial / NaN∪Inf checks. The "Display Filter" tab reverts to V2063's "Filter" naming and restores the "Hide Infinite" toggle.

== v20.65 ==
- Fixed crash on opening the Settings dialog: "cannot access free variable 'QLabel' where it is not associated with a value in enclosing scope".
  Cause: the Junk Filter tab builder method had two stray lines: `from PySide6.QtWidgets import QPlainTextEdit, QLabel` and `from PySide6.QtWidgets import QDoubleSpinBox`.
  QLabel / QPlainTextEdit / QDoubleSpinBox were already imported at module top; re-importing them inside a method made them locals of that method,
  which broke every nested closure that referenced them (textChanged / stateChanged callbacks etc.) with the above error.
  Fix: removed both method-local imports; reuse the module-top names.

== v20.62 ==

Every render frame the layout recomputes the minimum visible size from the current font / element spacing / bar / frame settings, then takes `max(auto_min, user_value)`. The card is never clipped regardless of how small the user sets the slider (the screenshot value 75×58 is automatically bumped up to a non-clipping minimum around 75×62).
- All-Buff module: the **"Rows" control has been removed from the Layout sub-tab**. Row count is now `ceil(buff_total / per_row)` and the window auto-resizes to the actual buff count. The **per-row count** control is preserved as the only layout knob.

== v20.39 ==
- Color-picker dialog "Custom colors" (16-cell palette) now persist across sessions. Previously, `QColorDialog.getColor()` popped the system ColorPicker, and after clicking "Add to Custom Colors" to fill the 16 cells, closing the app and re-launching wiped them all back to white (Qt's `QColorDialog` palette is in-process only). Fix: in `pick_color()`, **before** showing the dialog, restore the 16 stored hex colors from `settings['custom_palette']` via `QColorDialog.setCustomColor(i, QColor(hex))`; **after** the dialog closes (regardless of OK/Cancel), immediately read back `QColorDialog.customColors()` → save to `settings['custom_palette']` and call `save_settings`. The "Reset to Defaults" button now also clears the 16 cells and resets the Qt global. `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` gains a `custom_palette: []` field.
- Known limitations NOT fixed in V2039: ① "Add to Custom Colors" always fills the first empty cell — this is Windows system ColorPicker behavior, not customizable from Python; ② clicking an existing custom cell immediately overwrites the current selection with that cell's color — same reason. To get full flexibility would require writing a custom internal ColorPicker (~300 LOC); out of scope for V2039, see option A for a future revision.
- Version +1 (V2038 → V2039, title bar reports 20.39), schema 94 unchanged; no i18n key added/removed (only `pick_color` flow changed + one settings field added).

== v20.38 ==
- Fix the ability-name display for Id's dragon form (V2037 wrongly reused PL1900 human-form skill names for the dragon form, but the dragon form is a *separate* PL2000 with its own skill set). Cross-checked against GBFR Logs `lang/zh-CN/abilities.json`: Id's dragon form is a distinct PL2000 whose abilities are AB_PL2000_01~05 (Reginleiv Recidive / Scourge / Never Enough / Arcadia / Fourfold Vengeance), NOT the same as human-form PL1900 (Unbound / Atonement / Ragnarok Form). The project DB did not include PL2000, so the PL2000 hashes stored at `+ABILITY_HASH_OFFSET` on the dragon actor never matched `_ab_hash_map` → blank names. Fix: formally add PL2000's real abilities (hash→trilingual name) to `GBFR_Character_Skills_Buffs.json` so the hash lookup hits the real dragon-form names directly; and remove the erroneous PL1900→PL1900 / PL2000→PL1900 fallbacks added in V2037 (which would show wrong names). Core Buff area still shares PL1900 data (BUFF_PROFILES logic, unaffected).
- Version +1 (V2037 → V2038, title bar reports 20.38), schema 94 unchanged; no i18n key added/removed (data entries + data-path fix only).

== v20.37 ==
- **Real fix** for the bug where ability names under the Skill Module (skill_cd diamonds) disappeared when Id transformed into dragon form. V2036 misdiagnosed the cause (tried to make `read_skill_cooldowns` read the ability hash from the true-body actor via `_resolve_id_actor`, but the game does NOT necessarily update `+ABILITY_HASH_OFFSET` on the true body during dragon form — so V2036 still ended up reading 0 or stale values, hence "still nothing"). V2037 finally traces the root cause and fixes it: in dragon form, `pl_id` is resolved by `read_overlay_data` via `charid_hash` to **"PL1900"** (the true-body character ID — `CHAR_TYPE_TO_PL[0x20]="PL1900"`, NOT PL2000). The 4 hashes at `+ABILITY_HASH_OFFSET` on the dragon-form actor are *dragon-form-specific* hashes (not in the database), so `_ab_hash_map.get(h)` is a guaranteed miss. The original `PL_SKILL_FALLBACK` only listed PL0100/PL2000, **PL1900 was NOT in the fallback table → `_lookup_ability` returned None → `_skill_name=""` → `_draw_skill_cd_name` returned → all 4 diamonds rendered nameless**. Fix: add `"PL1900":"PL1900"` to `PL_SKILL_FALLBACK`, so dragon-form hash misses can borrow the true-body PL1900 ab_01-04 by slot (Reginleiv Recidive / Unbound / Atonement / Ragnarok Form). Also reverted V2036's `_resolve_id_actor` change inside `read_skill_cooldowns` (wrong root cause; restore direct `char_base` read). PL1900 true-body path is completely unaffected (first branch hits and returns the same true-body ability names). (Note: V2037's approach was wrong and is superseded by V2038.)
- Version +1 (V2036 → V2037, title bar reports 20.37), schema 94 unchanged; no i18n key added/removed (V2037 only touches the hash fallback table `PL_SKILL_FALLBACK`, no UI strings).

== v20.36 ==
- Fixed the bug where ability names under the Skill Module (skill_cd diamonds) disappeared when Id transformed into dragon form. Root cause: `read_skill_cooldowns` reads 4 ability hashes directly from `char_base + ABILITY_HASH_OFFSET`, but in dragon form `char_base` is the dragon-form actor (0x20) whose 4 hashes differ from the true body PL1900; `_lookup_ability(pl_id="PL1900", ab_hash=dragonhash)` misses, `_skill_name` returns empty, and `_draw_skill_cd_name` returns immediately. `read_overlay_data` already uses `_resolve_id_actor` for the same issue on ExStatus; `read_skill_cooldowns` was the one stragglers left.
- Fix: inside `read_skill_cooldowns`, add `_resolve_id_actor` to read the ability hash from the true body actor instead; cd cooldown still reads `char_base` (dragon actor's own cooldown state — left untouched to avoid introducing new risk). Normal form, Overdrive, and all other characters are unaffected. Minimal, traceable fix.
- Version +1 (V2035 -> V2036, title bar reports 20.36), schema 94 unchanged; no i18n key added/removed (V2036 only touches the back-end data path, no UI string changes).

== v20.34 ==
- Split spike-visibility and bead-visibility into two fully independent toggles. V2033 used two 'hide' options (hide spikes+beads / hide spikes only) whose combined semantics were convoluted and coupled. This build replaces them with the most straightforward design: two checkboxes under the 'Spikes & Ring' card — 'Show spikes (triangle bodies)' and 'Show decorative beads (spike-tip dots)' — each toggled independently, giving 4 free combinations: both on (default, everything shown) / beads only / spikes only / both off (bare ring + timer + text). Implementation simply forces the relevant layer's color opacity to 0 or its configured value; no coupled combo logic remains.
- Old saves auto-migrate: previously 'hide spikes+beads' -> both off; 'hide spikes only' -> spikes off, beads on; neither -> both on.
- Version +1 (V2033 -> V2034, title bar reports 20.34), schema 94 unchanged.

[Bugfix 2026-08-27 09:30 re-issued, same version number]
- User screenshots reported two rendering bugs still in `_draw_spikes` after the V2034 split; rewrite of that function fixed both:
  · bug 1: buff with zero stacks still painted 7 phantom decorative beads at every max_stacks slot even though no spike exists to attach them. Decorative beads depend on spikes; no spike -> no bead.
  · bug 2: when only 'Show decorative beads' is checked and 'Show spikes' is unchecked, **both** spikes and beads vanished from the screen. Root cause: `painter.setOpacity(self._effective_opacity(key))` wrote the spike-color opacity to the **global** painter; when `show_spikes=False` this resolves to 0, and the subsequent `_draw_spike_bead` call inherits the polluted state, making beads invisible.
- Fixed by rewriting `_draw_spikes` with a single clean flow:
  · `draw_count <= 0` -> return immediately (no spikes, no phantom beads either)
  · Per-layer loop with three branches: A (draw triangle + bead together with local `setOpacity(spike_opacity)`, including flash) / B (draw beads only with explicit `setOpacity(1.0)` to recover from the polluted state) / C (both toggles off -> draw nothing).
  · `_BUILD_NO` unchanged (still 2034), schema 94 unchanged.

== v20.33 ==
- New option "Hide spikes only (keep beads)" (Settings → Spike module, right after "Hide spikes & beads").
  · Difference vs "Hide spikes & beads": the latter also hides the root decorative beads; the former only hides the outward spike triangles while keeping the beads in place — for players who want "just the ring + timer + text + beads, no spike triangles".
  · Implementation: DEFAULT_SETTINGS adds `hide_spikes_only`: False; extracted `_draw_spike_bead` helper, called inside `_draw_spikes`' `hide_only` branch to draw beads only; new `_hide_spikes()` helper merges the two flags (hide_spikes_and_beads OR hide_spikes_only) used at the canvas-size / empty-branch / _render_buff_ui call sites; the outline's bead stroke is also kept in only mode, matching the spike internals.
  · Both can be checked together (no UI mutual-exclusion); checking both is equivalent to "Hide spikes & beads" (the hide_all early-return in `_draw_spikes` already blocks both).
  · Version +1 (V2032 -> V2033, title bar reports 20.33), schema 94 unchanged.

== v20.32 ==
- Reverted three chained over-fixes: V2026 misapplied `_any_active_buff_stacks()` to the spike-hidden check; V2027 aligned the title-bar buff-name segment to the same logic; V2031 added it to the render_core outer-if as well. Each time the user reported "everything is gone" or "only the buff-name tag is left", the AI tried to nail all three sites down with the same wrong predicate. This version rewinds all three back to the V2025 semantics, which only look at the `active_buffs` list itself, not at `stacks` values:
  · line 4226 `spike_hidden` check: `not self._any_active_buff_stacks()` → `not self.active_buffs`
  · line 4255 `render_core` outer-if: `if self.active_buffs and self._any_active_buff_stacks():` → `if self.active_buffs:`
  · line 5028 `_build_titlebar_status_text` title-bar buff-name segment check: `if not self._any_active_buff_stacks():` → `if not self.active_buffs:`
- Player intent: only when `active_buffs` is truly empty (no configured buff at all) does the whole module hide; "configured buff but live game stacks=0" should keep rendering the ring + spikes + timer + buff name normally.
- "Hide spikes and decorative beads" option stays as-is: only short-circuits the spike / bead draw path (`_draw_spikes` early-return), no effect on ring / timer / text.
- "Hide spike-circle module when no buff" option stays as-is: only when `active_buffs` is empty does it push every SPIKE_HIDDEN_KEYS entry to 0% opacity (full module invisible when spike_hide_when_no_buff=True + active_buffs=[]).
- V2031's "title->circle spacing spinbox now accepts negative values" is kept.
- Version +1 (V2031 -> V2032, title bar reports 20.32), schema 94 unchanged.

== v20.31 ==
- Fixed two regression bugs (per user screenshot reports: "title->circle spacing" spinbox rejecting negatives + buff modules' UI vanishing but leaving only the buff-name tag floating in air):
  · **"Title -> Circle Spacing" (`circle_pad_title`) QSpinBox range changed from `setRange(0, 999)` to `setRange(-999, 999)`**, allowing negative values -- needed when the user wants the spike-circle canvas to overlap on top of the title bar (`base_cy = TITLE_BAR_H + circle_pad_title + ...` already uses `circle_pad_title` as an active render parameter; V2030 wrongly suspected it was unused but the >=0 lower bound was meaningless, removing it <=0 allows the canvas to slide up over the title bar).
  · **Fixed a chain bug introduced when V2026 changed `_any_active_buff_stacks()`**: previously `if self.active_buffs:` entered the buff branch unconditionally, so when `active_buffs` was non-empty but every buff had `stacks == 0`, the circle / spikes / center icon / timer arc -- all of which belong to `SPIKE_HIDDEN_KEYS` -- got clamped to `spike_hidden_opacity` (default 0%, fully invisible), while `_draw_buff_name` uses `buff_name_color` (NOT in `SPIKE_HIDDEN_KEYS`) so the buff name tag kept rendering -- leaving the surreal visual of a perfectly drawn buff module whose circle/spikes/icon/timer are all invisible but a buff-name tag still floating in mid-air.
  · Outer condition changed to `if self.active_buffs and self._any_active_buff_stacks():` -- when the list is non-empty but every buff's stacks=0 it now falls through to the empty branch (identical to the empty-list case), and the "hide spike circle when no buff" option controls "fully hidden / show empty circle" uniformly. Three places are now consistent: (1) spike hidden (since V2026), (2) buff module rendering branch (this version), (3) title-bar buff-name segment (already used `_any_active_buff_stacks()` since V2027).
- Version +1 (V2030 -> V2031, title bar reports 20.31), schema 94 unchanged.

== v20.27 ==
- Fixed a bug where, after enabling "Hide spiked circle when no buff", the buff-name segment in the title bar status text (e.g. "Seofon - Crux: Edge of the Vanguard - (DMG ↑)") still rendered while the spike circle had become transparent. (Seofon user report: "after I turn off the spike hide option, all that's left in the game is the buff name, the rest is gone.") Root cause: V2026 changed the spike visibility check in `render_core` to `not self._any_active_buff_stacks()`, but line 5486 in `_build_titlebar_status_text` was still using the old `if not self.active_buffs` check, so when a buff was fully specced but had stacks=0 the circle hid but the buff name lingered. V2027 aligns line 5486 with the same predicate so the buff-name segment hides in lockstep with the spike circle.
